Anthropic shares fall 8.5% as investors scrutinize AI economics

Category: AI

Shares of Anthropic, the maker of the Claude chatbot and the first major frontier-AI lab to trade publicly, fell 8.5% to $88.91 on Friday, its first full trading day, after briefly approaching the $83 IPO price [15][30]. Anthropic reported third-quarter sales of $17 billion, up 47% from the previous quarter, and a $23.29 billion loss. Revenue growth slowed from 144% quarter over quarter in the second quarter, and compute spending grew faster than sales, according to the company’s filing and financial analysis [32][38]. Wall Street analysts are not expected to begin coverage for several weeks, and Anthropic’s first earnings report as a public company is months away. Its amended IPO filing remains a key source of company-specific financial information in the near term [32]. Index-tracking funds could also buy as much as 30% of the company’s shares by year-end, according to analytics firm Intropic [71].

Primary Source: View original article

We favor patience on Anthropic and recommend evaluating the company as it reports public results.

— Kaush Amin, head of private market investing at U.S. Bank Asset Management (Deccan Chronicle)

Key Points

  • Volatility gauge: The VIX rose to 40, its highest level since April 2025 [9].
  • Issuer proceeds: Of the $93.5 billion public offering, Anthropic received about $91.6 billion and selling shareholders received the balance [196].
  • Policy concerns: Candidates who backed slowing data-center construction won in several federal and state contests, adding to worries that political opposition could constrain the AI buildout [25][76].
  • Large customers: More than 11,900 customers spending over $100,000 with Anthropic accounted for about 74% of its revenue in the first nine months of 2026 [152].
  • Insider proceeds: Anthropic’s seven founders each sold about 3.4 million shares in the IPO, receiving roughly $280 million apiece before fees and taxes, according to TechCrunch’s analysis of the offering [182].
  • Limited float: Only 7.4% of Anthropic shares were estimated to be freely tradable, rising to as much as 9.8% when employee shares outside the founders’ group are counted [136].
  • Retail trading: Individual investors traded $772 million of Anthropic stock on debut day but were net buyers of only $60 million, below their first-day net purchases of SpaceX [12][150].
  • Limited allocations: Individual investors received about 15% of the offering, while roughly half of institutional accounts that applied received no shares, the Financial Times reported [120].
  • Investor access: Retail investors traded $772 million of Anthropic shares on its debut, a record in Vanda Research’s data going back to 2012, but their net purchases totaled $60 million—below the first-day totals for SpaceX and Coinbase [12].
  • Retail trading: Individual investors traded $772 million in Anthropic shares on debut day but were net buyers of only $60 million, according to Vanda Research [12].
  • Potential demand: Index-rebalance firm Intropic estimated that funds tracking stock indexes could buy up to 30% of Anthropic shares by year-end, although near-term volatility may persist [46][71].
  • Employee equity: About 5,100 of Anthropic’s 5,300 employees held company equity valued above $1 million, according to data cited by BetaNews [136].
  • Global losses: Taiwan’s market fell about 17% over the week, its steepest weekly drop since 1990, while Japan’s Nikkei and South Korea’s Kospi each lost more than 5% Friday [150][98].
  • Potential index demand: Index-tracking funds could account for purchases of up to 30% of Anthropic shares by year-end, Intropic estimates [46][50].
  • AI infrastructure spillover: The rout reached beyond chips, with optical-products maker Corning and energy provider Bloom Energy also falling as selling spread across companies tied to the buildout [25].
  • Trading options: Market makers estimated that a 20% intraday share-price move could imply 100-to-150 volatility for Anthropic options, which were expected to list Monday [4].

Perspectives

Neil Wilson, investor content strategist at Saxo Bank, said Anthropic’s performance could affect expectations for the wider AI ecosystem and the timing of OpenAI’s delayed public listing. He questioned whether OpenAI can justify a valuation of $1.5 trillion or more [47].

Sources: The National

Bob O’Donnell, chief analyst at TECHnalysis Research, said the IPO alone cannot establish stability. He cited the outlook for OpenAI, Nvidia, data-center politics and Anthropic’s performance as factors to watch [67].

Sources: Aaj English TV

Jacob Coxon, former Anthropic researcher: Coxon urged Anthropic to refuse work on recursive self-improvement, accusing the company of planning to pursue it as early as next year [146].

Sources: Tech Xplore (AFP)

Kaush Amin, head of private market investing at U.S. Bank Asset Management: Amin advised investors to wait for Anthropic’s public financial reporting, saying its current valuation assumes years of rapid growth [71].

Sources: Business Standard

Dario Amodei, Anthropic chief executive: Amodei said the company hopes eventually to obtain an investment-grade credit rating and intends to use debt financing when appropriate [73].

Sources: BusinessWorld

ARK Invest: Chief Executive Cathie Wood said the firm had owned Anthropic since early 2023, bought about $90.8 million in shares across six funds after the listing and was “averaging down” on the investment [37].

Sources: CNBC

Renaissance Capital: Investment-strategy director Avery Marquez said the IPO had heightened concerns about AI valuations, particularly because Anthropic was newly listed [24].

Anthropic: Chief Executive Dario Amodei said the company hopes to obtain an investment-grade credit rating and may use debt financing when appropriate [105].

Sources: Central News Agency

Bill Northey, senior investment director at U.S. Bank Wealth Management: Northey said short-term sentiment was dominating the session but argued that economic strength and company earnings tend to matter more over time [33].

Sources: Reuters via Yahoo Finance

Michael Antonelli, market strategist at Baird: Antonelli said investors were pricing in an end to the idea that AI companies could spend without limit to support share prices. He said he did not consider that proof the wider bull market had ended [177].

Sources: CBS News

Timeline

  • 26 October 2026: Anthropic filed its IPO registration statement and prospectus [34]
  • 19 November 2026: Anthropic began Nasdaq trading after pricing its IPO at $83 [15]
  • 20 November 2026: Shares fell 8.5% in Anthropic’s first full trading session [15]
  • 23 November 2026: Anthropic options were expected to begin trading Monday [37]
  • 2021: Former OpenAI researchers founded Anthropic with a stated AI-safety focus [158]
  • 30 September 2026: Anthropic reported revenue and computing commitments through the third quarter [152]
  • 19 November 2026: Anthropic began trading publicly on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT [130]
  • 20 November 2026: Shares fell to near the IPO price during the second trading session [3][137]
  • 2021: Former OpenAI employees founded Anthropic [177].
  • 18 November 2026: Anthropic priced shares at $83, above its $70-to-$78 target range [158].
  • 19 November 2026: Anthropic shares began trading on Nasdaq after the record IPO [30].
  • 20 November 2026: With the full greenshoe option exercised, Morgan Stanley could no longer use open-market purchases to cover any remaining overallotment if shares fell below $83 [15].
  • 6 October 2026: Nasdaq reached a record high before the ensuing selloff [219]
  • November 2026: Midterm election results heightened concerns over data-center construction [25][76]
  • 19 November 2026: Anthropic began trading after its record-setting public offering [76][33]
  • 20 November 2026: U.S. indexes fell sharply; S&P and Dow entered correction territory [25][177]

Industry Impact

  • AI cloud providers: CoreWeave fell 12.1% and Nebius 16.1% Friday as investors questioned data-center expansion and the financing behind AI infrastructure [101].
  • IPO market: OpenAI has indicated it may go public next year, while other high-profile technology and infrastructure firms have pushed IPO plans to 2027 [71].
  • Industry ties: AMD, Google, Microsoft, Nvidia and other technology companies participated in Anthropic’s concurrent Class N placement [30].
  • Semiconductors: The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell 10.7% Friday, with AMD, Micron and Broadcom among the chipmakers posting double-digit declines [129][98].
  • AI model companies: Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei described safety and AI advancement as a central tension for the company, which now faces shareholder pressure to generate profits [54].
  • Cloud and chip suppliers: Anthropic’s spending commitments link its expansion to suppliers including Amazon, Google Cloud and Microsoft, which also have investment ties; cloud marketplaces generated about 44% of its revenue in the first nine months of 2026 [152].

Historical Background

The S&P 500’s previous correction began in March 2025, when President Donald Trump’s trade war rattled markets [25].

Business Angle

  • Some infrastructure suppliers depend on third-party financing; Anthropic warned that financing difficulties could delay, reprice, reduce or prevent delivery of contracted computing capacity [152].
  • A data-center lease issue gives either party the option to terminate Anthropic’s roughly $34.9 billion agreement with Lambda, although neither had issued a termination notice and Anthropic said it did not plan to terminate [152].
  • Intropic estimates index-tracking funds could buy up to 30% of Anthropic’s shares by year-end [71].
  • Investors are assessing whether a valuation of about 40 times trailing sales can be sustained [71].

Q&A

Q: What do options-market estimates suggest about trading risk?

A: A 20% intraday move points to implied volatility of 100% to 150%. That estimate comes against S&P 500 volatility near 30, compared with 17.3 when SpaceX debuted, suggesting Anthropic options could be unusually costly [37].

Q: Can Morgan Stanley resume buying shares to support the price?

A: With the full greenshoe exercised, Morgan Stanley can no longer use open-market purchases to cover any remaining overallotment if shares fall below the $83 IPO price [15].
